两个月过去，一切都显得没多大指望了，克林特却照旧忙活，照旧存着盼头。只要有一星作用，他都肯把老马弄进屋里、在炉子边给他铺个窝；说实在的，但凡能让老小马眼里透出一点活气，叫他干什么都成。可他能做的都做了；每回他把手掌搭上那瘦骨嶙峋的老脖子、摸着那张老皮，他都忍不住骂娘，恨不得把那些造下这孽的人挨个拧得变了形。骂着骂着，他的脸色又会变，想起从前、把眼前这副老残骸跟那匹马当年的模样一比，他差点儿放声哭出来。



===Original===
As much as Clint had liked Smoky, the old wreck of a shadow of that horse wasn’t wanting for any of the same liking. It was still in the cowboy’s heart a plenty, and if anything, more so on account that the old pony was now needing help, and a friend like he’d never needed before; and Clint was more on hand with the horse, now that he was worthless, than he’d been when Smoky was the four hundred dollar cowhorse and worth more.
===Chinese===
克林特当年有多喜欢烟儿，如今这匹马的影子和残骸，也丝毫不缺这份喜欢。那份情分在牛仔心里还满着呢；非但没减，反倒更重了——因为这老小马眼下正需要帮衬，需要一个他从未这样需要过的朋友；这马如今一钱不值了，克林特守在他跟前的时候，反倒比烟儿还是那匹四百美元、身价更高的牧牛马那时更多。



===Original===
Finally, and after many a day of care and worrying, Clint begin to notice with a glad smile that the pony’s hide was loosening up. Then after a week or so more of shoving hay and grain, condition powers, and other things down the old pony’s throat, a layer of meat begin to spread over them bones and under that hide. Then one day a spark showed in the pony’s eyes, soon after that he started taking interest in the things around.
===Chinese===
终于，在许多天的照料和揪心之后，克林特开始带着欢喜的笑容注意到，小马的皮松快了。又往那老喉咙里塞了一个来星期的干草、谷物、养膘药粉和别的零七八碎之后，一层肉开始往那些骨头上、在那张皮下铺展开来。跟着有一天，小马眼里亮起了一星火花；没过多久，他对身边的事物来了兴致。



===Original===
As layer after layer of meat and then tallow accumulated and rounded the sharp corners of Smoky’s frame, that pony was for noticing more and more till after a while his interest spread enough, and with a clearer vision, went as far as to take in the man, who kept a going and coming, once in a while touched him, and then talked.
===Chinese===
一层又一层的肉、接着又是油膘积起来，把烟儿骨架上那些尖棱棱的角都磨圆了，这小马留意的东西也越来越多，过了一阵，他的兴致铺展开去，眼神也清亮起来，竟一路注意到那个人身上去了——那人整天走来走去，时不时摸摸他，还跟他说说话。



===Original===
Clint liked to had a fit one day, when talking to the horse and happened to say *Smoky*, he noticed that pony cock an ear.
===Chinese===
有一天克林特险些没背过气去：他正跟马说话，无意间说了声*烟儿*，却见那小马竖起了一只耳朵。



===Original===
The recuperating of the horse went pretty fast from then on; and as the winter days howled past and early spring drawed near, there was no more fear of Smoky’s last stand being anywheres near. As the days growed longer and the sun got warmer, there was times when Clint would lead the horse out and turn him loose to walk around in the sunshine, and that way get the blood to circulating. Smoky would sometimes mosey along for hours around the place and then start out on some trail, but always when the sun went down, he was by the stable door again and then Clint would let him in.
===Chinese===
打那以后，这马的将养就快起来了；随着冬日的风嚎着过去、早春临近，再不必担心烟儿的最后一搏近在眼前了。日子渐长，日头渐暖，克林特时常把马牵出去撒开，让他在太阳底下四处遛遛，好活血。烟儿有时绕着这地方慢悠悠走上几个钟头，然后顺着哪条小径走上一程，可太阳一落山，他准又回到了厩门口，克林特便放他进去。



===Original===
Clint would watch him by the hour whenever the horse was out that way, and he’d wonder, as he kept his eye on him, if that pony remembered, if the knocks he’d got from different people in different countries, didn’t forever make him forget his home range and all that went with it. Not many miles away was where he was born; the big mountains now covered with snow was the same he was raised on, and which he tore up with his hoofs as he played while a little colt, and by his mammy. The corrals by the stable and sheds was the ones he was first run into when branded, and in them, a few years later, broke to saddle; but what Clint would wonder the most, as he watched, is whether Smoky remembered *him*.
===Chinese===
每当马在外面那样转悠，克林特能一连几个钟头看着他，眼睛盯着他，心里犯嘀咕：这小马还记得吗？他在不同的地方、挨不同的人下的那些狠手，会不会早让他把家乡的草场和草场上的一切忘了个干净？离这儿没几英里，就是他落生的地方；如今覆着积雪的大山，正是他从小长大的那几座——还是小马驹的时候，他在马妈妈身边撒欢，蹄子把山皮刨了个遍。马房和棚子边上的畜栏，就是他头一回被赶进去打烙印的那几座；几年之后，也是在那里，他被调教上了鞍子。可克林特望着他时最犯嘀咕的，还是烟儿记不记得*他*。



===Original===
The cowboy had kept a hoping that sometime he’d be greeted with a nicker as he’d open the stable door in the morning. Clint felt if the horse remembered, he would nicker that way at the sight of him and like he used to; but morning after morning went by, and even though Smoky seemed full of life and rounded out to near natural again, no nicker was ever heard.
===Chinese===
这牛仔一直存着个盼头：哪天早晨他打开厩门，能迎上一声低嘶。克林特觉得，这马要是还记得，一见他准会像从前那样低嘶一声；可一个早晨接着一个早晨过去，烟儿看上去已经精力充盈、圆润得快回原样了，那声低嘶却始终没有响过。



===Original===
“Somebody must of stretched that pony’s heartstrings to the breaking point,” he remarked one day, as he’d stopped, wondering as usual, and looked at the horse.
===Chinese===
「准是有人把这小马的心弦绷到断了，」有一天他站住脚，照例犯着嘀咕，望着马说道。



===Original===
Finally spring came sure enough, and broke up the winter. Green grass-covered ridges took the place of snow banks, and the cottonwoods along the creeks was beginning to bud. It was during one of them fine spring days, when riding along and looking the country over, Clint run acrost a bunch of horses. In the bunch was a couple of colts just a few days old, and knowing that old ponies have such a strong interest and liking for the little fellers, the cowboy figgered the sight of ’em would help considerable in bringing Smoky’s heart up a few notches, and maybe to remembering. He fell in behind the bunch and hazed ’em all towards the corrals, and as Smoky, turned loose that day, spotted the bunch, his head went up. Then he noticed the little fellers, and that old pony, gathering all the speed there was in him, headed straight for the bunch and amongst ’em.
===Chinese===
春天终于真真切切地来了，把冬天拆了个干净。绿草覆顶的山脊换下了雪堆，溪边的三角叶杨也开始冒芽。就在这样一个大好的春日，克林特骑马遛着、四下检看这片地方，撞见了一小群马。群里有几匹才落生几天的小马驹。老马对这帮小家伙素来最有兴味、最疼爱，牛仔盘算着，让他们入眼一瞧，准能把烟儿的心劲往上提几格，说不定还能勾起他的记性。他兜到马群后头，把整群往畜栏里赶。那天撒在外头的烟儿一望见马群，脑袋就昂了起来。跟着他又瞅见了那几个小家伙，这匹老小马攒起浑身仅剩的速度，直奔马群、扎进了群里。



===Original===
Clint corralled him and all the rest together and setting on his horse at the gate, watched Smoky while that horse was having the time of his life getting acquainted. The pony dodged kicks and bites and went back and forth thru the bunch, and a spark showed in his eye which hadn’t been there for many a day.
===Chinese===
克林特把他和其余的马一齐圈进了栏，自己骑在马上守在门口，瞧着烟儿——这马正过着他此生最痛快的结识时光。小马躲闪着蹄子啃咬，在马群里穿来穿去，眼里闪着一星多日不见的火花。



===Original===
The cowboy could near see the horse smile at the little colts; and he was surprised at the show of action and interest the old pony had reserved, or gained. He was acting near like a two-year-old, and Clint grinned as he watched.
===Chinese===
那牛仔几乎能看见这马朝小马驹们绽开的笑；老小马暗攒下的、或是新养出来的这股劲头和兴致，也让他吃了一惊。他撒起欢来简直像匹两岁驹，克林特瞧着，咧嘴直乐。



===Original===
“Daggone his old hide,” says the cowboy, “it looks to me like he’s good to live and enjoy life for many summers yet”; then thinking strong, he went on, “and maybe in that time he might get to remembering me again﻿—I wonder.”
===Chinese===
「这老东西的皮，」牛仔道，「照我看，他还够活着、够享受好多个夏天呢」；接着他思绪翻腾，又说，「兴许在这段日子里，他还能重新记起我来——难说啊。」



===Original===
He watched Smoky a while longer and till he got acquainted some, and at last deciding it’d be for the best to let him go, he reined his horse out of the gate and let the bunch run by. The old pony seemed to hesitate some as the bunch filed out. He liked their company mighty well but something held him back; then a horse nickered, and even though that nicker might not of been meant for him, it was enough to make him decide. He struck out on a high lope and towards the bunch. One of the little colts and full of play waited for him, and nipping the old horse in the flanks, run by his side till the bunch was caught up with﻿—Smoky was *living* again.
===Chinese===
他又看了一阵烟儿，直到他混了个脸熟，末了拿定主意还是放他走最好，便拨转马头出了栏门，放马群跑过去。马群鱼贯而出时，老小马像是迟疑了一下。他极喜欢跟他们做伴，可有什么东西拽着他；这时一匹马低嘶了一声——那声低嘶未必是冲他来的，却足够让他拿定主意。他放开大跑，直追马群。一匹小马驹正玩得起劲，等着他过来，一口叼住老马的肋腹，咬着跟他并排跑，直到追上大群——烟儿又*活*过来了。



===Original===
Clint sat on his horse and watched the bunch lope out over a ridge and out of sight; and with a last glimpse at the mouse colored rump he grinned a little, but it was a sorry grin, and as he kept a looking the way Smoky had gone, he says:
===Chinese===
克林特坐在马上，望着马群大跑着翻过一道山脊、消失在视线外；最后瞥了一眼那鼠灰色的屁股，他微微咧了咧嘴，可那是苦笑。他一直望着烟儿跑去的方向，说道：



===Original===
“I wonder if he ever will.”
===Chinese===
「也不知他到底还会不会。」



---



			



===Original===
With the green grass growing near an inch a day, Clint wasn’t worried much on how old Smoky was making it. He figgered a horse couldn’t die if he wanted to, not on that range at that time of the year; but some day soon he was going to try and locate the old horse and find out for sure how he really was. Then a lot of work came on which kept the cowboy from going out soon as he wanted to, and then one morning, bright and early, as he stepped out to get a bucket of water, the morning sun throwed a shadow on the door; and as he stuck his head out a nicker was heard.
===Chinese===
青草一天长将近一英寸，老烟儿的日子过得怎么样，克林特倒不怎么担心。他盘算着，在那片草场上、在那个节令，一匹马想死都死不成；不过过不了几天，他还是打算去寻寻这匹老马，弄清楚他到底怎样了。接着一大摊子活压上来，牛仔想走也一时走不开。后来一天清晨，天刚透亮，他跨出门去提一桶水，朝阳往门上投下一道影子；他探出头去，只听一声低嘶。



===Original===
Clint dropped his bucket in surprise at what he heard and then seen. For, standing out a ways, slick and shiny, was the old mouse colored horse. The good care the cowboy had handed him, and afterwards, the ramblings over the old home range, had done its work. The heart of Smoky had come to life again, and full size.
===Chinese===
克林特听见那声、又瞧见眼前的一切，手里的桶惊得掉在了地上。就在外头不远处站着的，正是那匹鼠灰色的老马，皮毛油光水滑。牛仔给他的那番好生照料，加上随后在家乡草场上的信步漫游，都见了效。烟儿的心活了过来——而且是完完整整、足尺足寸地活了过来。

## Preface / 序言

===Original===
To my way of thinking there’s something wrong, or missing, with any person who hasn’t got a soft spot in their heart for an animal of some kind. With most folks the dog stands highest as man’s friend, then comes the horse, with others the cat is liked best as a pet, or a monkey is fussed over; but whatever kind of animal it is a person likes, it’s all hunkydory so long as there’s a place in the heart for one or a few of them.

I’ve never yet went wrong in sizing up a man by the kind of a horse he rode. A good horse always packs a good man, and I’ve always dodged the hombre what had no thought nor liking for his horse or other animals, for I figger that kind of gazabo is best to be left unacquainted with. No good would ever come of the meeting.

With me, my weakness lays towards the horse. My life, from the time I first squinted at daylight, has been with horses. I admire every step that crethure makes. I know them and been thru so much with ’em that I’ve come to figger a big mistake was made when the horse was classed as an animal. To me, the horse is man’s greatest, most useful, faithful, and powerful friend. He never whines when he’s hungry or sore footed or tired, and he’ll keep on a going for the human till he drops.

The horse is not appreciated and never will be appreciated enough﻿—few humans, even them that works him, really know him, but then there’s so much to know about him. I’ve wrote this book on only one horse and when I first started it I was afraid I’d run out of something to write, but I wasn’t half thru when I begin to realize I had to do some squeezing to get the things in I wanted; and when I come to the last chapter was when I seen how if I spent my life writing on the horse alone and lived to be a hundred I’d only said maybe half of what I feel ought to be said.

The horse I wrote of in this book is not an exception, there’s quite a few like him. He’s not a fiction horse that’s wrote about in a dream and made to do things that’s against the nature of a horse to do. Smoky is just a horse, but all horse; and that I think is enough said.

As for Clint, the cowboy who “started” Smoky, he’s no exception either. He’s just a man who was able to see and bring out the good that was in the horse﻿—and no matter how some writers describe the cowboy’s handling of horses, I’m here to say that I can produce many a cowboy what can show feelings for a horse the same as Clint done.

But Smoky met other humans besides Clint, many others, and of all kinds, and that’s where the story comes in. And now, my main ambition as I turn Smoky loose to making hisself acquainted is that the folks who will get to know him see that horse as *I* seen him.

Will James

===Chinese===
依我看，一个人心里要是对任何一种动物都没有一处柔软的地方，那这人准是哪儿不对劲，或缺了点什么。在多数人心里，狗居人之友的首位，其次是马；也有人最爱拿猫当宠物，或者宠一只猴子；但不管一个人喜欢的是什么动物，只要心里装得下一只或几只，那就万事都好。

凭人骑什么样的马来看人，我从来没走过眼。好马驮的准是好汉；而对那些对自己的马或其他动物毫无心思、毫无喜爱的人，我一向躲着走——依我看，那种家伙最好别去结识。跟他们打照面，从来不会有好事。

至于我，我的偏爱在马身上。打我头一回睁开眼看天光起，我这辈子就没离开过马。那牲口走的每一步我都欣赏。我了解它们，又同它们一起经历过那么多事，以至于我渐渐觉得，当初把马归入畜类实在是个天大的错误。在我眼里，马是人类最伟大、最有用、最忠诚、最有力的朋友。它饿了、脚疼了、累了，从不呜咽；为了人，它能一直走到自己倒下为止。

马没有得到足够的珍视，将来也永远不会得到足够的珍视——没几个人，连使唤马的人在内，真正懂马；可马的学问又那么多。我这本书只写了一匹马，动笔之初还怕没的可写，可写到一半就开始发觉，想把要写的东西都装进去，还得使劲挤才行；等写到最后一章我才明白，就算我一辈子只写马这一样东西，活到一百岁，我心底想说的话恐怕也只说出一半。

我书里写的这匹马并非绝无仅有，像它这样的还颇有几匹。它不是那种梦呓里编出来、被逼着做违背马之天性之事的虚构马。烟儿就是一匹马，但浑身是马气；我想，有这一句也就说尽了。

至于克林特，那个「调教出」烟儿的牧牛郎，他也不是什么例外。他只是一个能看出并发扬马身上优点的人——不管某些作家怎么描写牛仔对待马的手段，我在此要说，我能找出许许多多像克林特一样对马有真感情的牛仔。

不过除了克林特，烟儿还遇见过许多别的人，形形色色的许多人，故事正打这儿来。如今，在我放烟儿出去结交朋友之际，我最大的心愿就是：将要认识它的读者们，能像我一样看这匹马。

威尔·詹姆斯（Will James）
